from pyomo.environ import value
from tools.solver_helpers import load_model, solve_model
import pyomo.environ as pyo

# Load baseline model and solve to capture original solution
base = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)

description_base = (
    "Baseline run: solving the original 'chem' model to record feasibility of acetone production "
    "and the presence/absence of chemicals before any what-if changes. This provides a reference "
    "for comparing the impact of removing hydronium ion (chemical 5)."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(base, 'chem__baseline', models_dictionary, tool_context, description_base)

# Extract baseline results
base = load_model('chem__baseline', models_dictionary)
status_base = str(base.component_data_objects(pyo.Suffix))  # fallback; we'll get solver status via Pyomo attributes if available
obj_base = pyo.value(base.obj)

# Collect baseline chemical presence vector
baseline_y = {int(i): int(round(pyo.value(base.y[i]))) for i in base.C}

# Inspect which reactions can produce acetone (chemical 6), and the required reactants for each
acetone_producing_reactions = []
if hasattr(base, 'RP'):
    for (r, prod) in base.RP:
        if prod == 6:
            reactants = [int(c) for c in base.C if (r, 6, c) in base.rm and int(base.rm[r,6,c]) == 1]
            acetone_producing_reactions.append((r, reactants))

# Build the scenario: set chemical 5 to unavailable (y[5] = 0)
scen = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)  # fresh copy from base

# Deactivate any existing material availability constraint that fixes y[5]
try:
    if 5 in scen.material_cons:
        scen.material_cons[5].deactivate()
except Exception:
    pass

# Add explicit scenario constraint to force unavailability of chemical 5
scen.y_5_set0 = pyo.Constraint(expr=scen.y[5] == 0)

description_scen = (
    "What-if analysis: set chemical 5 (hydronium ion) to unavailable by enforcing y[5]==0. "
    "Purpose: test whether acetone (chemical 6) can still be produced and how the logical synthesis "
    "network responds when reaction 2's required hydronium is removed."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(scen, 'chem__y_5_set0', models_dictionary, tool_context, description_scen)

# Extract scenario results
scen = load_model('chem__y_5_set0', models_dictionary)
obj_scen = pyo.value(scen.obj)

# Collect scenario chemical presence vector
scenario_y = {int(i): int(round(pyo.value(scen.y[i]))) for i in scen.C}

# Prepare a compact summary object to return
result = {
    'baseline_objective_y6': int(round(obj_base)),
    'scenario_objective_y6': int(round(obj_scen)),
    'baseline_y': baseline_y,
    'scenario_y': scenario_y,
    'acetone_producing_reactions': acetone_producing_reactions,
}

import pyomo.environ as pyo

# Helper functions available in REPL: load_model, solve_model

# 1) Solve baseline model to capture original solution
base = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)

description_base = (
    "Baseline run: solve the original 'chem' model to establish whether acetone (chemical 6) is "
    "synthesizable with the default availability of chemicals. This provides reference objective "
    "and chemical presence (y) before the what-if change to hydronium (chemical 5)."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(base, 'chem__baseline', models_dictionary, tool_context, description_base)

base = load_model('chem__baseline', models_dictionary)
obj_base = pyo.value(base.obj)

# Extract baseline chemical presence
baseline_y = {int(i): int(round(pyo.value(base.y[i]))) for i in base.C}

# Find which reactions can produce acetone (chemical 6) and their reactants
acetone_producing_reactions = []
for (r, prod) in base.RP:
    if prod == 6:
        reactants = [int(c) for c in base.C if (r, 6, c) in base.rm and int(base.rm[r,6,c]) == 1]
        acetone_producing_reactions.append((r, reactants))

# 2) Scenario: set chemical 5 (hydronium ion) unavailable: y[5] == 0
scen = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)
# Deactivate any existing material constraint fixing y[5]
try:
    if 5 in scen.material_cons:
        scen.material_cons[5].deactivate()
except Exception:
    pass
# Enforce unavailability of chemical 5
scen.y_5_set0 = pyo.Constraint(expr=scen.y[5] == 0)

description_scen = (
    "What-if analysis: enforce unavailability of chemical 5 (hydronium ion) by adding y[5]==0 and "
    "deactivating any conflicting availability constraint. Purpose: assess if acetone (chemical 6) "
    "remains producible, how reaction 2 and alternative pathways respond, and quantify impacts on key y variables."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(scen, 'chem__y_5_set0', models_dictionary, tool_context, description_scen)

scen = load_model('chem__y_5_set0', models_dictionary)
obj_scen = pyo.value(scen.obj)
scenario_y = {int(i): int(round(pyo.value(scen.y[i]))) for i in scen.C}

# Diagnose viability of reaction 2 before/after: all its reactants present?
# Identify reactants for reaction 2 producing acetone (prod=6)
rxn2_reactants = []
for (r, prod) in base.RP:
    if r == 'r02' and prod == 6:
        rxn2_reactants = [int(c) for c in base.C if (r, 6, c) in base.rm and int(base.rm[r,6,c]) == 1]

rxn2_viable_base = (len(rxn2_reactants) > 0) and all(baseline_y.get(c,0)==1 for c in rxn2_reactants)
rxn2_viable_scen = (len(rxn2_reactants) > 0) and all(scenario_y.get(c,0)==1 for c in rxn2_reactants)

# Which alternative reactions for acetone (prod=6) remain viable in scenario (all reactants present)?
alt_viable_base = []
alt_viable_scen = []
for (r, reactants) in acetone_producing_reactions:
    viable_base = all(baseline_y.get(c,0)==1 for c in reactants)
    viable_scen = all(scenario_y.get(c,0)==1 for c in reactants)
    if viable_base:
        alt_viable_base.append(r)
    if viable_scen:
        alt_viable_scen.append(r)

# Compute before/after changes in y
changes = []
for i in sorted(list(base.C)):
    if baseline_y[i] != scenario_y[i]:
        changes.append({'chemical': i, 'before': baseline_y[i], 'after': scenario_y[i]})

result = {
    'objective_baseline_y6': int(round(obj_base)),
    'objective_scenario_y6': int(round(obj_scen)),
    'baseline_y6': baseline_y.get(6, None),
    'scenario_y6': scenario_y.get(6, None),
    'rxn2_reactants_for_acetone': rxn2_reactants,
    'rxn2_viable_baseline': bool(rxn2_viable_base),
    'rxn2_viable_scenario': bool(rxn2_viable_scen),
    'acetone_producing_reactions_all': [r for (r, _) in acetone_producing_reactions],
    'acetone_viable_reactions_baseline': alt_viable_base,
    'acetone_viable_reactions_scenario': alt_viable_scen,
    'y_changes': changes,
}
result

2026-02-16 21:10:45.969 | INFO     | optichat.tools.python_repl:python_repl_func:90 - Python code to execute:
import pyomo.environ as pyo

# Load and solve baseline
base = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)

description_base = (
    "Baseline run: solve original 'chem' to record acetone (y[6]) and chemical availability before "
    "removing hydronium (chemical 5). Establishes a reference for the what-if."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(base, 'chem__baseline', models_dictionary, tool_context, description_base)

base = load_model('chem__baseline', models_dictionary)
obj_base = pyo.value(base.obj)
baseline_y = {int(i): int(round(pyo.value(base.y[i]))) for i in base.C}

# Identify reactions that can produce acetone (chemical 6) and their required reactants
acetone_producing_reactions = []  # list of (reaction_id, [reactant_ids])
for (r, prod) in base.RP:
    if prod == 6:
        reactants = [int(c) for c in base.C if (r, 6, c) in base.rm and int(pyo.value(base.rm[r,6,c])) == 1]
        acetone_producing_reactions.append((r, reactants))

# Determine how 'reaction 2' is labeled in data (e.g., '02' vs 'r02') by picking the reaction that has an ID ending with '2'
# Prefer exact '02' if present
rxn2_id = None
for (r, prod) in base.RP:
    if prod == 6 and (r == '02' or r == 'r02' or r == '2'):
        rxn2_id = r
        break
# Fallback: try to find any reaction id whose digits equal 2
if rxn2_id is None:
    for (r, prod) in base.RP:
        if prod == 6:
            digits = ''.join([ch for ch in str(r) if ch.isdigit()])
            if digits == '2':
                rxn2_id = r
                break

rxn2_reactants = []
if rxn2_id is not None:
    rxn2_reactants = [int(c) for c in base.C if (rxn2_id, 6, c) in base.rm and int(pyo.value(base.rm[rxn2_id,6,c])) == 1]

rxn2_viable_base = (len(rxn2_reactants) > 0) and all(baseline_y.get(c,0)==1 for c in rxn2_reactants)

# Build scenario: force y[5] = 0 (hydronium unavailable)
scen = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)
# Deactivate existing availability constraint on 5 if present
try:
    if 5 in scen.material_cons:
        scen.material_cons[5].deactivate()
except Exception:
    pass
scen.y_5_set0 = pyo.Constraint(expr=scen.y[5] == 0)

description_scen = (
    "What-if: enforce y[5]=0 (hydronium unavailable) to see if acetone (y[6]) can still be produced. "
    "Checks reaction 2 viability and alternative acetone pathways under this change."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(scen, 'chem__y_5_set0', models_dictionary, tool_context, description_scen)

scen = load_model('chem__y_5_set0', models_dictionary)
obj_scen = pyo.value(scen.obj)
scenario_y = {int(i): int(round(pyo.value(scen.y[i]))) for i in scen.C}

# Viability of acetone-producing reactions before/after
alt_viable_base = []
alt_viable_scen = []
for (r, reactants) in acetone_producing_reactions:
    viable_base = all(baseline_y.get(c,0)==1 for c in reactants)
    viable_scen = all(scenario_y.get(c,0)==1 for c in reactants)
    if viable_base:
        alt_viable_base.append(r)
    if viable_scen:
        alt_viable_scen.append(r)

rxn2_viable_scen = False
if rxn2_id is not None:
    rxn2_viable_scen = all(scenario_y.get(c,0)==1 for c in rxn2_reactants) if rxn2_reactants else False

# Track changes in y
changes = []
for i in sorted(list(base.C)):
    if baseline_y[i] != scenario_y[i]:
        changes.append({'chemical': i, 'before': baseline_y[i], 'after': scenario_y[i]})

result = {
    'objective_baseline_y6': int(round(obj_base)),
    'objective_scenario_y6': int(round(obj_scen)),
    'baseline_y6': baseline_y.get(6, None),
    'scenario_y6': scenario_y.get(6, None),
    'rxn2_id': rxn2_id,
    'rxn2_reactants_for_acetone': rxn2_reactants,
    'rxn2_viable_baseline': bool(rxn2_viable_base),
    'rxn2_viable_scenario': bool(rxn2_viable_scen),
    'acetone_producing_reactions_all': [r for (r, _) in acetone_producing_reactions],
    'acetone_viable_reactions_baseline': alt_viable_base,
    'acetone_viable_reactions_scenario': alt_viable_scen,
    'y_changes': changes,
}
result

What we changed and how we ran it
- We kept the original model structure and added a single constraint y[5] = 0 to represent “hydronium not available,” while deactivating any pre-existing availability fix for chemical 5. We solved:
  - Baseline: chem__baseline
  - Scenario with hydronium removed: chem__y_5_set0

Key background on this model
- Variables y[c] are binary “present/not present” indicators for chemicals. There are no reaction flow or extent variables; reactions are logical rules that link the presence of reactants to the presence of products.
- Acetone is chemical 6; the objective minimizes y[6]. If the model can avoid producing acetone, it sets y[6] = 0; if any reaction’s reactants are all present and that reaction produces acetone, the logic forces y[6] = 1.

Chemicals that can produce acetone (6) in this model
- Reaction '02': needs chemicals 4 and 5
- Reaction '06': needs chemicals 11, 12, and 13
- Reaction '08': needs chemicals 15, 16, and 17
- Reaction '09': needs chemicals 12, 18, and 19

Results

1) Feasibility
- Status after removing hydronium: feasible and optimal.
- No constraints became infeasible. The model found a solution that still satisfies all logical reaction rules and availability constraints with y[5] forced to 0.

2) Objective and solution
- Objective (minimize y[6], i.e., try to avoid acetone): unchanged at 1.0.
  - Interpretation: The network still “must” produce acetone under the remaining available chemicals because at least one acetone-producing route remains fully enabled.
- Acetone presence (y[6]): baseline 1 → scenario 1 (no change; acetone is still present).
- “Reaction 2” (the route using hydronium):
  - It requires chemicals 4 and 5. With hydronium removed (y[5] = 0), this route cannot operate (not all required reactants are present).
  - Note: Even in the baseline, this route wasn’t actually the binding path to acetone because chemical 4 was not present then (y[4] = 0).
- What produces acetone instead:
  - Reaction '06' (needs 11, 12, 13) remains fully enabled:
    - Scenario y[11] = 1, y[12] = 1, y[13] = 1
    - Therefore this route still forces y[6] = 1.
- Other notable y changes:
  - Chemical 5 (hydronium): 1 → 0 (enforced change)
  - Chemical 4: 0 → 1 (solver selected a different but still optimal pattern for non-critical chemicals; because there is no penalty for extra “presence,” multiple optimal solutions exist. This does not change the conclusion that reaction '02' is still inoperative because 5 is 0.)
  - All other chemicals stayed the same in value relative to the baseline optimal solution we inspected.

3) Alternatives to reaction 2 for acetone
- Viable under the scenario (y[5] = 0):
  - Reaction '06' (uses 11, 12, 13): viable (all present) → this is the route enforcing acetone presence.
- Not viable under the scenario:
  - Reaction '02': needs 4 and 5; hydronium 5 is 0 → not viable.
  - Reaction '08': needs 15, 16, 17; 15 and 16 are 0 → not viable.
  - Reaction '09': needs 12, 18, 19; 18 and 19 are 0 → not viable.

4) Sensitivity / minimal adjustments
- Because the objective and feasibility did not worsen, no fix is needed to maintain acetone production.
- If you specifically want acetone via reaction 2 (the hydronium route), the minimal practical changes are:
  - Restore hydronium supply: set y[5] from 0 back to 1.
  - Ensure the other required reactant (chemical 4) is present. Two ways:
    - Directly supply chemical 4 (set y[4] = 1), or
    - Enable the precursor path that creates 4 via reaction '01' by ensuring y[1] = 1 (since y[2] and y[3] are already 1). With y[1], y[2], y[3] all present, reaction '01' forces y[4] = 1.
- If instead you wanted to disable acetone entirely (make y[6] = 0), you would need to break all acetone routes. The current binding route is reaction '06', so making any one of chemicals 11, 12, or 13 unavailable (set to 0) would be sufficient. Practically, that means withholding supply of, for example, chemical 13 (HCl), if that’s acceptable.

- Model status: optimal (feasible)
- New objective: 1.0 (unchanged)

  - The active acetone pathway remains reaction '06' (needs 11, 12, 13), unchanged in effect.

  - To keep acetone production with 5 unavailable: no action needed; reaction '06' suffices.

1) What I changed and how the model works
- Change applied: chemical 5 (hydronium) was set to unavailable (forced to 0).
- Important model detail: the model uses y[...] variables to indicate whether a chemical is present (1) or absent (0). Reactions are represented by logical rules that make a product chemical present when all its required reactants are present. There are no continuous reaction‑extent flows in this model — presence/absence is what the solver controls.
- Objective: the model minimizes y[6] (i.e., it tries to avoid acetone if possible). The baseline snapshot had objective = 1.0.

2) Feasibility
- Result: The model remains feasible and solved to optimality after forcing chemical 5 = 0. No constraints became infeasible.
- Reason: Even without hydronium, at least one alternate acetone pathway remains enabled so the solver found a valid assignment of presence/absence for all chemicals that satisfies every logical constraint.

3) Objective and key solution changes
- New objective value: 1.0 (unchanged).
  - Interpretation: the model still ends up with acetone present (y[6] = 1) in the optimal solution — removing hydronium did not allow the model to avoid acetone.
- Chemical presence values of interest:
  - y[5] (hydronium): 1 → 0 (forced change).
  - y[6] (acetone): 1 → 1 (unchanged; acetone still present).
  - y[4]: observed change 0 → 1 in the scenario (this is a non‑critical flip due to multiple optimal solutions; it does not enable reaction 2 because y[5] = 0).
- Reaction 2 (the hydronium route that produces acetone):
  - Status: cannot operate in the scenario because it requires both chemical 4 and chemical 5; with chemical 5 = 0 the reaction is disabled.
  - Note: in the baseline reaction 2 was already not the binding path (chemical 4 was 0 baseline), so removing hydronium simply eliminates one potential route that was not the root cause of acetone’s presence.
- Which reactions increased/decreased to compensate:
  - The active acetone-producing pathway that remains and enforces y[6] = 1 is reaction '06' (it requires chemicals 11, 12, and 13). That route remains enabled and is the reason acetone stays present.

  - Reaction '08' and '09' — remain not viable (still missing required reactants in both baseline and scenario).
  - Aside from y[4] flipping 0→1 in the solver’s chosen optimal pattern, other chemical presence values did not materially change.

4) Alternatives (can acetone still be produced any other way?)
- Yes. Reaction '06' (needs chemicals 11, 12, 13) is available and is the active route producing acetone in the scenario.
- Other acetone routes:
  - '02' (needs 4 and 5) — disabled because 5 = 0.
  - '08' (needs 15, 16, 17) — not enabled (missing reactants).
  - '09' (needs 12, 18, 19) — not enabled (missing reactants).
- So the model switches (or relies) on reaction '06' to produce acetone once hydronium is removed.

- If you want acetone production to continue but specifically via reaction 2 (the hydronium route):
  - Minimal change: restore hydronium (set chemical 5 back to 1). Also ensure chemical 4 is available (set y[4] = 1) — chemical 4 can be provided directly or enabled by enabling the precursor reaction that makes 4.

- If you want other specific trade-offs (e.g., force reaction 2 but avoid reaction 06), consider combinations: enable 5 and 4 and then remove one of 11/12/13.
- Note on magnitude estimates: because presence is modeled as binary, the “amount” adjustments are discrete (flip availability on/off). There is no continuous small‑supply quantity in this model to report; the minimal practical changes are therefore presence toggles (0 ↔ 1).

import json
import pyomo.environ as pyo

# Helper names mapping from model file comment
chem_names = {
 1:'ch3co2c2h5', 2:'naoc2h5', 3:'c2h5oh', 4:'ch3coch2co2c2h5', 5:'h3o-hydronium ion', 6:'ch3coch3',
 7:'co2', 8:'ch3cn', 9:'ch3mgi', 10:'c2h5oc2h5', 11:'ch3c(nmgi)ch3', 12:'h2o', 13:'hcl', 14:'ch3cho',
 15:'ch3ch(oh)ch3', 16:'cro3', 17:'h2so4', 18:'ch2=c(ch3)2', 19:'o3', 20:'hco2h', 21:'ch3i', 22:'mg',
 23:'ch3co2ch3', 24:'hoc(ch3)3', 25:'ch4', 26:'i2', 27:'hi', 28:'o2', 29:'cr2o3', 30:'ch3cl', 31:'nacn',
 32:'nacl', 33:'cl2', 34:'ch3cooh'
}

# Utility to extract reactants for a given reaction r and product p
def required_reactants(model, r, p):
    return [i for i in model.C if (r, p, i) in model.rm and int(pyo.value(model.rm[r, p, i])) == 1]

# Utility to compute reaction enabled status given y values
def reaction_enabled(yvals, reactants):
    return all(int(yvals[i]) == 1 for i in reactants)

# Load and solve baseline
model = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)

description_base = (
    "Baseline solve for what-if analysis: no parameter changes. Purpose is to record the reference "
    "objective value (minimize y[6], acetone presence) and baseline availability-driven propagation of "
    "chemical presences via reaction constraints. This establishes which acetone-producing reactions are "
    "enabled under given material availability and provides a clear before-state for comparison when we "
    "modify the availability of chemical 31 (nacn)."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(model, 'chem__baseline_run1', models_dictionary, tool_context, description_base)

# Collect baseline y values
base_y = {i: int(round(pyo.value(model.y[i]))) for i in model.C}
base_obj = float(pyo.value(model.obj))

# Determine reactant sets for acetone-producing reactions and their baseline enabled status
acetone_product = 6
rxns = ['02','06','08','09']
reactant_sets = {r: required_reactants(model, r, acetone_product) for r in rxns}
base_enabled = {r: reaction_enabled(base_y, reactant_sets[r]) for r in rxns}

# Now create what-if: force y[31] == 0 (make nacn unavailable)
model2 = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)
# Deactivate the existing availability constraint that fixes y[31] == 1
if 31 in list(model2.material_cons):
    model2.material_cons[31].deactivate()
# Add a new constraint to force y[31] == 0
model2.y31_forced_zero = pyo.Constraint(expr = model2.y[31] == 0)

description_whatif = (
    "What-if analysis: force chemical 31 (nacn) to be unavailable by deactivating its material constraint "
    "and adding y[31]==0. Purpose: test whether acetone (y[6]) production can still be logically implied via "
    "any reaction path and measure the impact on objective (minimize y[6]) and on reaction enablement for "
    "reactions 2, 6, 8, and 9."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(model2, 'chem__y_31_set0', models_dictionary, tool_context, description_whatif)

# Collect after-change y values and objective
after_y = {i: int(round(pyo.value(model2.y[i]))) for i in model2.C}
after_obj = float(pyo.value(model2.obj))

# Reaction enablement after change uses the same reactant lists
after_enabled = {r: reaction_enabled(after_y, reactant_sets[r]) for r in rxns}

# Compute flips 0<->1
flips = []
for i in model.C:
    if base_y[i] != after_y[i]:
        flips.append({
            'index': i,
            'name': chem_names.get(i, f'y{i:02d}'),
            'before': base_y[i],
            'after': after_y[i]
        })

# Build reaction detail listing for Part A
partA_detail = []
for r in rxns:
    reqs = reactant_sets[r]
    partA_detail.append({
        'reaction': r,
        'product_index': acetone_product,
        'product_name': chem_names[acetone_product],
        'required_reactants': [{'index': j, 'name': chem_names.get(j, f'y{j:02d}')} for j in reqs],
        'contains_31': (31 in reqs)
    })

result = {
    'baseline': {
        'objective': base_obj,
        'y6': base_y[6],
        'reaction_enabled': base_enabled
    },
    'what_if_y31_0': {
        'objective': after_obj,
        'y6': after_y[6],
        'reaction_enabled': after_enabled
    },
    'objective_change': after_obj - base_obj,
    'y_flips': flips,
    'partA_reaction_details': partA_detail
}

print(json.dumps(result, indent=2))

  - Does reactant list include 31 (nacn)? No

Conclusion: nacn (index 31) is not a reactant nor a product in any of the four acetone-producing reactions (2, 6, 8, 9).

Part B — What-if run: force nacn unavailable (set y[31] = 0)

How I implemented it:
- Deactivated the built-in availability constraint fixing y[31] = 1.
- Added a new constraint y[31] = 0.
- Solved a baseline first (no changes), then solved the what-if case.

1) Model status
- Baseline: optimal (feasible)
- With y[31] = 0: optimal (feasible)

2) Objective value (minimize y[6] = acetone presence)
- Baseline objective: 1.0
- New objective: 0.0
- Change: -1.0 (objective improved because the model minimizes acetone presence)

3) Acetone (y[6]) and reaction 2/6/8/9 statuses
- Baseline:
  - y[6] = 1
  - Reaction enabled? 02: No, 06: Yes, 08: No, 09: No
- With y[31] = 0:
  - y[6] = 0
  - Reaction enabled? 02: No, 06: No, 08: No, 09: No

Interpretation: In the baseline, acetone was enabled via Reaction 06 (needs 11, 12, 13). When nacn is removed, CH3CN (8) cannot be produced via Reaction 18 (which needs 31), so intermediate 11 (ch3c(nmgi)ch3) collapses, and Reaction 06 becomes disabled. Since the objective is to minimize y[6], the model then sets acetone to 0.

4) Other notable presence flips (0 ↔ 1)
- 6 (ch3coch3, acetone): 1 → 0
- 8 (ch3cn): 1 → 0
- 11 (ch3c(nmgi)ch3): 1 → 0
- 31 (nacn): 1 → 0 (forced by what-if)
All other chemicals remained unchanged in this run.

5) If objective changes or feasibility is lost, how to restore performance?
Note: Under the current objective (minimize acetone presence), setting y[31]=0 “improves” the objective by eliminating acetone. If instead your practical goal is to keep acetone producible (y[6]=1), here are minimal adjustments:

- Fastest fix: restore nacn availability
  - Set y[31] = 1 (re-enable CH3CN via Reaction 18 → restores 11 → re-enables Reaction 06 → acetone y[6]=1)

- Alternate acetone paths that avoid nacn (keep y[31]=0):
  - Enable Reaction 02 path (needs 4 and 5):
    - Make cr2o3 available: set y[29] = 1
    - Why: y29 enables Reaction 16 (→14), then Reaction 22 (→34), then Reaction 21 (→1), then Reaction 01 (→4); with 5 already available, Reaction 02 can then produce acetone.
  - Enable Reaction 08 path (needs 15, 16, 17):
    - Set y[16] = 1 (cro3) and y[29] = 1 (cr2o3)
    - Why: y29 enables 14, which is needed in Reaction 07 to form 15; with 16 and existing 17, Reaction 08 can produce acetone.
  - Enable Reaction 09 path (needs 12, 18, 19):
    - Set y[19] = 1 (o3) and y[23] = 1 (ch3co2ch3)
    - Why: y23 allows Reaction 12 to form 24; then Reaction 13 forms 18; with 12 available and 19 restored, Reaction 09 can produce acetone.

Practical parameters to adjust (explicit values)
- To keep acetone via original path: y[31] = 1
- Or alternate paths:
  - R02 route: y[29] = 1
  - R08 route: y[16] = 1 and y[29] = 1
  - R09 route: y[19] = 1 and y[23] = 1

Brief non-technical summary
- nacn (31) is not directly used in acetone-making reactions 2, 6, 8, 9. However, it feeds an upstream step that creates CH3CN (8), which is needed to make intermediate 11 for Reaction 06. When we removed nacn, CH3CN and intermediate 11 disappeared, so acetone could no longer form via Reaction 06. The model (which minimizes acetone presence) then chose y[6]=0. To keep acetone producible, either restore nacn (y[31]=1) or enable another acetone route by supplying a small set of missing materials as listed above.

Part A — Is chemical 31 (nacn) involved directly in acetone reactions 2, 6, 8, 9?

  - Reaction 09 (→ acetone): needs chemicals 12, 18, and 19. Chemical 31 is not involved.

Part B — What-if: force nacn (chemical 31) unavailable (y[31] = 0)
What I did: forced y[31] = 0 and solved the model; I compared baseline and scenario.

1) Model status
- Baseline: optimal (feasible).
- With y[31] = 0: optimal (feasible).

2) Objective value
- Baseline objective: 1.0
- New objective (with y[31] = 0): 0.0
- Change: objective improved by 1.0 (it went from 1.0 to 0.0).

3) Acetone and reactions 2/6/8/9 (before → after)
- Acetone (y[6]): 1 → 0 (acetone present in baseline; absent in the scenario).
- Reaction availability status (enabled = all its reactants present):
  - Reaction 02: No → No (remained disabled; needs 4 and 5).
  - Reaction 06: Yes → No (was the active acetone path in baseline; disabled after removing nacn).
  - Reaction 08: No → No (remained disabled).
  - Reaction 09: No → No (remained disabled).

Why acetone disappeared: although 31 is not directly in reactions 2/6/8/9, it supplies an upstream chain that creates CH3CN (chemical 8) and then intermediate 11, which is a required reactant for reaction 06. Removing nacn collapses that upstream chain, disables reaction 06, and therefore the solver can avoid acetone — and because the model minimizes acetone presence, it sets y[6] = 0.

4) Other notable chemical presence flips (before → after)
- y[31] (nacn): 1 → 0 (forced).
- y[8] (ch3cn): 1 → 0 (lost because its precursor is gone).
- y[11] (intermediate feeding reaction 06): 1 → 0 (lost, which disables reaction 06).
- y[6] (acetone): 1 → 0 (as above).
- All other chemicals remained unchanged in this run.

5) If objective changes or feasibility is lost — recommendations
- In this case the objective improved (acetone eliminated) and feasibility was preserved, so no restoration is needed.
- If your operational goal was to keep acetone producible, minimal actions to restore acetone include:
  - Re-enable nacn: set y[31] = 1 (restores CH3CN → intermediate 11 → reaction 06 → acetone).
  - Or enable a different acetone path by supplying a small set of other chemicals:
    - To enable Reaction 02: ensure chemicals 4 and 5 are available (e.g., set relevant upstream suppliers active such as y[29] = 1 to create a chain that supplies 4, plus y[5]=1 if needed).
    - To enable Reaction 08: ensure 15, 16, 17 are available.
    - To enable Reaction 09: ensure 12, 18, 19 are available.
- These are discrete (presence/absence) fixes in this model; there is no continuous “small amount” variable to supply partial availability.

Short, actionable summary
- nacn (31) is not directly part of acetone reactions, but it is upstream of the active acetone route (reaction 06).
- Forcing nacn unavailable disables that upstream path and removes acetone, improving the model objective from 1.0 to 0.0.
- If you need acetone back, the simplest is to re-enable nacn (y[31] = 1) or turn on an alternative acetone-producing route by providing its necessary chemicals.

# Load and solve the base 'chem' model, inspect y[17], y[18], y[19], objective, and binding constraints for these chemicals
import pyomo.environ as pyo

# Helper functions are available per tool conventions
model = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)

# Record availability settings for chemicals 17, 18, 19
avail_c = set(model.material_cons._body().kwargs['avail_c']) if hasattr(model, 'material_cons') else set()
unavail_c = set(model.material_cons._body().kwargs['unavail_c']) if hasattr(model, 'material_cons') else set()
# Fallback: access through model construction variables if stored on model (not standard). We will instead inspect from model attributes if present.
try:
    avail_c = set(model.component('avail_c'))
except Exception:
    pass
try:
    unavail_c = set(model.component('unavail_c'))
except Exception:
    pass

# The parameters were created from 'data'; we can try to retrieve from model attributes saved during build time
if hasattr(model, 'avail_c'):
    avail_c = set(model.avail_c)
if hasattr(model, 'unavail_c'):
    unavail_c = set(model.unavail_c)

# For robust retrieval, we will look into model.material_cons to see which y[c] are fixed by equality constraints
fixed_eqs = {}
for c in list(model.C):
    con = model.material_cons[c]
    if con.body is None:
        continue
    # Identify if it is an equality setting y[c] == 1 or y[c] == 0
    body = pyo.value(con.body)
    # con is of form y[c] == 1 or y[c] == 0; we can detect RHS from con.lower/upper
    lb = con.lower
    ub = con.upper
    fixed_eqs[c] = (lb, ub)

# Solve the model (baseline)
description = (
    "Baseline solve: minimize y[6] (acetone present) under reaction-closure constraints and material availability. "
    "Extract baseline values for y[17], y[18], y[19] and check which reaction constraints bind for these products."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(model, 'chem__baseline', models_dictionary, tool_context, description)

# Reload the solved model to access values
m = load_model('chem__baseline', models_dictionary)

# Collect baseline y values
baseline = {i: pyo.value(m.y[i]) for i in [6,17,18,19]}

# Identify binding reaction constraints for prod in {17,18,19}
# A reaction constraint is binding if it is active (not skipped) and LHS == RHS within tolerance
binding = {17: [], 18: [], 19: []}
nonbinding = {17: [], 18: [], 19: []}
for (r, prod) in list(m.reaction_cons):
    con = m.reaction_cons[r, prod]
    if con.body is None or (prod not in [17,18,19]):
        continue
    if not con.active:
        continue
    lhs = pyo.value(con.body)
    rhs = con.lower if con.has_lb() else con.upper  # It's a >= constraint, so lower bound is RHS
    rhs = float(rhs) if rhs is not None else None
    if rhs is None:
        continue
    if abs(lhs - rhs) <= 1e-6:
        binding[prod].append(r)
    else:
        nonbinding[prod].append(r)

# Now, force y[17] = y[18] = y[19] = 1 and re-solve to see objective change and any binding constraints
m2 = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)
# Add forcing constraints using same style (Constraint indexed by C). We'll add a new ConstraintList for clarity.
if hasattr(m2, 'force_three_present'):
    m2.force_three_present.deactivate()
m2.force_three_present = pyo.ConstraintList()
for idx in [17,18,19]:
    m2.force_three_present.add(m2.y[idx] == 1)

description2 = (
    "What-if (WHY-NOT) analysis: forced chemicals 17 (H2SO4), 18 (isobutene), and 19 (ozone) to be present "
    "by adding equality constraints y[17]=y[18]=y[19]=1. Purpose: test whether simultaneous presence triggers "
    "additional reactions that force acetone presence (y[6]=1), and inspect which reaction constraints bind."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(m2, 'chem__force_17_18_19_set1', models_dictionary, tool_context, description2)

m2s = load_model('chem__force_17_18_19_set1', models_dictionary)
forced = {i: pyo.value(m2s.y[i]) for i in [6,17,18,19]}

# Check binding constraints for prod in {17,18,19} in forced case
binding2 = {17: [], 18: [], 19: []}
for (r, prod) in list(m2s.reaction_cons):
    con = m2s.reaction_cons[r, prod]
    if con.body is None or (prod not in [17,18,19]):
        continue
    if not con.active:
        continue
    lhs = pyo.value(con.body)
    rhs = con.lower if con.has_lb() else con.upper
    rhs = float(rhs) if rhs is not None else None
    if rhs is None:
        continue
    if abs(lhs - rhs) <= 1e-6:
        binding2[prod].append(r)

# Prepare a concise textual report
report = {
    'availability_fixed_bounds': {k: v for k, v in fixed_eqs.items() if k in [17,18,19]},
    'baseline_y': baseline,
    'baseline_binding_rxns_for_prod': binding,
    'forced_y': forced,
    'objective_baseline': pyo.value(m.obj),
    'objective_forced': pyo.value(m2s.obj),
    'forced_binding_rxns_for_prod': binding2,
}
report

import pyomo.environ as pyo

# Load and solve baseline model
model = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)

# Solve baseline
description = (
    "Baseline solve for WHY-NOT analysis: minimize y[6] under reaction-closure and availability constraints. "
    "Goal: record baseline y[6], y[17], y[18], y[19] and identify which reaction constraints are binding for acetone (prod=6)."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(model, 'chem__baseline', models_dictionary, tool_context, description)

m = load_model('chem__baseline', models_dictionary)

# Helper: gather reaction mapping rm for selected products
sel_prods = [6,17,18,19]
rm_map = {p: {} for p in sel_prods}  # rm_map[prod][r] = [reactants]
for r in list(m.R):
    for p in sel_prods:
        reactants = [c for c in list(m.C) if (r, p, c) in m.rm and int(pyo.value(m.rm[r,p,c]))==1]
        if reactants:
            rm_map[p][r] = reactants

# Evaluate baseline y values
baseline_y = {p: int(pyo.value(m.y[p])) for p in sel_prods}
obj_base = float(pyo.value(m.obj))

# Identify binding reaction constraints for prod=6 (acetone) and for 17,18,19
binding = {p: [] for p in sel_prods}
slacks = {p: {} for p in sel_prods}
for p in sel_prods:
    for r, reactants in rm_map[p].items():
        con = m.reaction_cons[r, p]
        if not con.active:
            continue
        lhs = sum(int(pyo.value(m.rm[r,p,c]))*(1 - pyo.value(m.y[c])) for c in reactants)
        rhs = 1 - pyo.value(m.y[p])
        slack = lhs - rhs  # for >= constraint
        slacks[p][r] = float(slack)
        if abs(slack) <= 1e-6:
            binding[p].append(r)

# Force y[17]=y[18]=y[19]=1 and re-solve; deactivate any potentially conflicting availability constraints first
m2 = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)
# Deactivate specific material_cons if they exist for indices 17,18,19
for idx in [17,18,19]:
    try:
        m2.material_cons[idx].deactivate()
    except Exception:
        pass
# Add forcing constraints
m2.force_three_present = pyo.ConstraintList()
for idx in [17,18,19]:
    m2.force_three_present.add(m2.y[idx] == 1)

description2 = (
    "WHY-NOT what-if: enforce y[17]=y[18]=y[19]=1 via ConstraintList after deactivating any availability constraints for these. "
    "Purpose: test whether simultaneous presence triggers acetone (y[6]=1) and which reaction constraints become binding."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(m2, 'chem__force_17_18_19_set1', models_dictionary, tool_context, description2)

m2s = load_model('chem__force_17_18_19_set1', models_dictionary)
forced_y = {p: int(pyo.value(m2s.y[p])) for p in sel_prods}
obj_forced = float(pyo.value(m2s.obj))

# Binding info in forced case for prods 6,17,18,19
binding2 = {p: [] for p in sel_prods}
slacks2 = {p: {} for p in sel_prods}
for p in sel_prods:
    for r, reactants in rm_map[p].items():
        con = m2s.reaction_cons[r, p]
        if not con.active:
            continue
        lhs = sum(int(pyo.value(m2s.rm[r,p,c]))*(1 - pyo.value(m2s.y[c])) for c in reactants)
        rhs = 1 - pyo.value(m2s.y[p])
        slack = lhs - rhs
        slacks2[p][r] = float(slack)
        if abs(slack) <= 1e-6:
            binding2[p].append(r)

# Prepare structured report
report = {
    'objective_baseline': obj_base,
    'objective_forced': obj_forced,
    'baseline_y': baseline_y,
    'forced_y': forced_y,
    'rm_map_subset': {p: {r: reactants for r, reactants in rm_map[p].items()} for p in sel_prods},
    'binding_rxns_baseline': binding,
    'binding_rxns_forced': binding2,
    'slacks_baseline': slacks,
    'slacks_forced': slacks2,
}
report

2026-02-16 21:24:54.214 | INFO     | optichat.tools.python_repl:python_repl_func:90 - Python code to execute:
import pyomo.environ as pyo
m = load_model('chem__baseline', models_dictionary)

info = {}
for c in [17,18,19,6]:
    yv = int(pyo.value(m.y[c]))
    con = m.material_cons[c]
    fixed = None
    if con.active and con.equality:
        # equality implies y[c] == const
        rhs = float(con.lower) if con.has_lb() else None
        fixed = rhs
    # list reactions that produce c (i.e., rm[r,c,react]==1 for some react)
    rxn_map = {}
    for r in list(m.R):
        reacts = [rc for rc in list(m.C) if (r,c,rc) in m.rm and int(pyo.value(m.rm[r,c,rc]))==1]
        if reacts:
            rxn_map[r] = reacts
    # Identify any binding reaction constraints for this product
    bind = []
    for r, reacts in rxn_map.items():
        con2 = m.reaction_cons[r,c]
        if not con2.active:
            continue
        lhs = sum(int(pyo.value(m.rm[r,c,rc]))*(1-int(pyo.value(m.y[rc]))) for rc in reacts)
        rhs = 1 - int(pyo.value(m.y[c]))
        if abs(lhs - rhs) <= 1e-6:
            bind.append((r, lhs, rhs))
    info[c] = {
        'y_value': yv,
        'material_fixed_to': fixed,
        'producing_reactions': rxn_map,
        'binding_rxns': bind,
    }

obj = float(pyo.value(m.obj))
{'objective': obj, 'info': info}

2026-02-16 21:29:14.209 | INFO     | optichat.tools.timing_tracker:record_llm_call:134 - ⏱️  [expert_agent] Operation #8: LLM call #8: 111.688s
2026-02-16 21:29:14.209 | INFO     | optichat.tools.callback_tool:check_llm_response:829 - [Callback] Inspecting LLM response from 'expert_agent': Short answer up front
- Baseline solution values: y[17]=1, y[18]=0, y[19]=0; objective y[6]=1 (minimized value is 1.0).
- Why not all three? Two reasons:
  1) Ozone (19) is explicitly unavailable: material_cons[19]: y[19] == 0 (binding).
  2) Minimizing acetone (6) discourages having both isobutene (18) and ozone (19) on together with water (12), because reaction_cons['09',6] would then force acetone on. The optimizer avoids completing that reactant set.

Details and exact model pieces
1) What the model is doing
- Variables: y[c] ∈ {0,1} indicate whether chemical c is present.
- Objective: obj = minimize y[6]. The model prefers to keep acetone (chemical 6) off unless constraints force it on.
- Reaction-closure logic: For each reaction r producing product p with reactants in rm[r,p,·], the constraint is:
  sum over reactants c of rm[r,p,c]·(1 − y[c]) ≥ (1 − y[p]).
  Plain-language: “If product p is present (y[p]=1), every one of its reactants must also be present (y[c]=1).” Equivalently, “If all reactants are present, the product must be present.”

2) What we observe in the baseline (from the solved model “chem__baseline”)
- Objective: MINIMIZE y[6], optimal value = 1.0
- Key y values:
  - y[17] = 1 (sulfuric acid; forced present)
  - y[18] = 0 (isobutene; off)
  - y[19] = 0 (ozone; forced absent)
- Binding constraints that matter:
  - Availability constraints:
    • material_cons[17]: y[17] == 1 (binding)
    • material_cons[19]: y[19] == 0 (binding) → ozone is disallowed
    • material_cons[12]: y[12] == 1 (binding) → water is always on
  - Reaction logic limiting 18:
    • reaction_cons['13',18]: 1 − y[18] ≤ rm['13',18,17](1 − y[17]) + rm['13',18,24](1 − y[24]) (binding)
      Intuition: to allow y[18]=1, you need y[17]=1 and y[24]=1. Here y[17]=1 but y[24]=0, so the model is content to keep y[18]=0.
    • reaction_cons['12',24]: 1 − y[24] ≤ rm['12',24,9](1 − y[9]) + rm['12',24,23](1 − y[23]) (binding)
      Intuition: to turn on y[24], you need y[9]=1 and y[23]=1. Baseline has y[9]=1 but y[23]=0, so y[24] stays 0 and therefore y[18] also stays 0.
  - Paths that would force acetone:
    • reaction_cons['09',6]: 1 − y[6] ≤ rm['09',6,12](1 − y[12]) + rm['09',6,18](1 − y[18]) + rm['09',6,19](1 − y[19]) (not binding in baseline)
      If all three reactants 12, 18, and 19 were present, this would force y[6]=1. Because the objective minimizes y[6], the model avoids turning on 18 if 19 were allowed; in baseline, it’s moot because 19 is prohibited.

3) Why having 17, 18, 19 all present is discouraged
- A hard prohibition on 19: material_cons[19]: y[19] == 0. This single equality rule blocks ozone (19). That alone prevents the trio {17,18,19}.
- Objective pressure against completing the 09 path: Given water is on (material_cons[12]: y[12]==1), if both 18 and 19 were also on, reaction_cons['09',6] would force acetone on. Because the model minimizes y[6], it will avoid creating a complete set of {12,18,19}. In baseline this is enforced partly by the unavailability of 19; if 19 were available, the model would still prefer to keep 18 off unless needed elsewhere.
- Additional logical hurdle for 18: reaction_cons['13',18] requires y[17]=1 and y[24]=1 to allow y[18]=1. While y[17] is fixed at 1, y[24] is off; to turn y[24] on, you need y[23]=1 via reaction_cons['12',24]. Since y[23]=0 in the baseline and there is no objective reward for turning 23/24 on, y[18] stays off. In short, there’s a chain of prerequisites (24 then 23) with no benefit under the objective.

4) Binding constraints now vs. if you force all three on
- Baseline binding among the trio:
  - material_cons[17] (y[17]==1), material_cons[19] (y[19]==0), reaction_cons['13',18] (keeps y[18]=0 due to y[24]=0), and material_cons[12] (y[12]==1).
- If you force y[17]=y[18]=y[19]=1, reaction_cons['09',6] will bind because all its reactants {12,18,19} are then present, which compels y[6]=1. We verified in a what-if run (chem__force_17_18_19_set1) that the objective remains 1.0; i.e., it does not worsen the objective in this dataset because y[6] was already 1.

5) Minimal changes to make y[17], y[18], y[19] all present
- Change availability for ozone (19):
  - Relax/modify material_cons[19]: move 19 from the “unavailable” set to “available,” or remove that equality and set y[19]==1. Concretely, change unavail_c to exclude 19 or add a new constraint y[19]==1.
- Enable the isobutene (18) route by satisfying its prerequisites:
  - Since reaction_cons['13',18] requires y[24]=1 (and y[17]=1, already true), you need to make y[24]=1. The simplest ways:
    1) Make y[23]==1 so reaction_cons['12',24] forces y[24]==1 (y[9] is already 1), or
    2) Directly set y[24]==1 via availability if allowed.
- Objective/cost implication:
  - In this baseline, the objective is already y[6]=1. Forcing 17, 18, 19 to 1 does not change the objective; it stays 1.0. If, in a different data setting, y[6] could be 0, turning on both 18 and 19 (with 12 fixed at 1) would activate reaction_cons['09',6] and force y[6] to 1, increasing the objective by +1.

6) Practical knobs you can adjust (with explicit values)
- Availability parameters (material constraints):
  - Set y[19] from 0 to 1: change material_cons[19] to y[19]==1 (or move 19 from unavail_c to avail_c).
  - Set y[23] from 0 to 1: add material_cons[23]: y[23]==1 (so that reaction_cons['12',24] then forces y[24]==1).
  - Optionally, directly set y[24]==1 if enabling 23 is not desired.
- If you also want 17, 18, 19 present but still keep acetone off (y[6]=0), you must break one element of reaction 09:
  - Either relax material_cons[12] and set y[12]=0, or remove one of the reaction-09 mappings rm['09',6,18] or rm['09',6,19]. Otherwise reaction_cons['09',6] will force y[6]=1 when 12,18,19 are all 1.

References to exact components
- Objective: obj = MINIMIZE: y[6]
- Availability constraints (binding): material_cons[12]: y[12]==1; material_cons[17]: y[17]==1; material_cons[19]: y[19]==0
- Discouraging reaction path to acetone: reaction_cons['09',6]: 1 − y[6] ≤ rm['09',6,12](1 − y[12]) + rm['09',6,18](1 − y[18]) + rm['09',6,19](1 − y[19]) (not binding in baseline; would bind if 12,18,19 all 1)
- Prerequisite for 18: reaction_cons['13',18]: 1 − y[18] ≤ rm['13',18,17](1 − y[17]) + rm['13',18,24](1 − y[24]) (binding)
- To turn on 24: reaction_cons['12',24]: 1 − y[24] ≤ rm['12',24,9](1 − y[9]) + rm['12',24,23](1 − y[23]) (binding)

Bottom line
- It’s “not recommended” (i.e., not chosen by the optimizer) to have 17, 18, 19 all present because:
  - 19 is flat-out prohibited by availability.
  - The objective minimizes acetone, so it avoids completing any reaction path that would force acetone on; 12 is fixed on, so making both 18 and 19 present would complete reaction 09 and pin y[6]=1.
  - Turning on 18 also requires turning on 24, which in turn needs 23; there’s no objective benefit to doing that, so the optimizer keeps 18 off.

If you want all three on, change material_cons[19] to 1 and enable 23 (or directly 24); the objective remains 1.0 in this dataset.

2026-02-16 21:29:30.211 | INFO     | optichat.tools.callback_tool:check_llm_response:829 - [Callback] Inspecting LLM response from 'root_agent': [WHY_NOT]

Short answer
- The model avoids having chemicals 17, 18, and 19 all present because: (a) chemical 19 (ozone) is explicitly prohibited by an availability constraint, and (b) if 12 (water), 18 and 19 were all present they would enable reaction 09 which forces acetone (6) on — and the objective minimizes acetone. Also, turning on 18 requires additional upstream chemicals (24 and 23) with no benefit under the current objective, so the optimizer prefers to keep 18 off.

Baseline facts (from the solved model)
- Baseline values: y[17] = 1, y[18] = 0, y[19] = 0.
- Baseline objective: y[6] = 1 (objective value = 1.0).

Which exact constraints / rules cause this behavior (plain language plus model pointers)
1) Hard availability rule for ozone
- material_cons[19]: y[19] == 0.
  - This is a direct equality in the model that forbids ozone (19). That alone prevents the trio {17,18,19}.

2) Objective discourages completing the acetone path through reaction 09
- Objective: minimize y[6] (the model wants acetone off if possible).
- Reaction-closure logic for reaction 09 (model form): 
  - reaction_cons['09',6] roughly enforces: if reactants 12, 18, and 19 are all present then product 6 must be present.
  - Plain-language: because water (12) is already fixed on, turning on both 18 and 19 would complete reaction 09 and force y[6]=1. The optimizer avoids enabling 18 or 19 together with 12 because that would increase the objective (if y[6] could otherwise be kept 0).

3) Upstream prerequisites for 18 (isobutene)
- reaction_cons['13',18] ties y[18] to y[17] and y[24]: it requires y[24]=1 (and y[17]=1) before y[18] can be turned on.
- reaction_cons['12',24] ties y[24] to y[9] and y[23]; baseline has y[9]=1 but y[23]=0, so y[24] stays 0 and this blocks y[18].
  - Plain-language: to get 18 you must also turn on 24 and 23 (additional chemicals). There’s no objective benefit to activating those extra chemicals, so the solver leaves 18 off.

What is binding in the baseline
- material_cons[17]: y[17] == 1 (present)
- material_cons[19]: y[19] == 0 (absent — binding)
- material_cons[12]: y[12] == 1 (present)
- reaction_cons['13',18] (keeps y[18] off because y[24]=0)
- reaction_cons['12',24'] (keeps y[24] off because y[23]=0)

What happens if you force all three on (y[17]=y[18]=y[19]=1)
- I tested that scenario: forcing all three to 1 is possible if you relax material_cons[19] and satisfy prerequisites for 18 (e.g., set y[23]=1 or y[24]=1). In the model’s dataset I checked, forcing them all did not change the objective (it remained 1.0) because acetone was already present in baseline.
- Important general point: if acetone had been avoidable (y[6]=0) and you then enabled 12, 18, and 19 together, reaction 09 would force acetone on and the objective would increase by +1 (since objective is binary y[6]).

Minimal changes to make all three present (concrete model actions)
1) Allow ozone:
  - Change material_cons[19] so y[19] can be 1 (remove or relax the equality y[19]==0; or set y[19]==1 explicitly).
2) Enable isobutene (18) prerequisites:
  - Option A (preferred chain): set y[23] = 1 (this will allow reaction_cons['12',24] to set y[24] = 1, enabling reaction_cons['13',18'] to permit y[18]=1).
  - Option B: directly set y[24] = 1 (if allowed), then set y[18] = 1.
3) Make sure 17 is already present (baseline y[17]=1 so no change needed).
- Summary command-style: set y[19]=1 and y[23]=1 (or y[24]=1); the model will then allow y[18]=1.

Objective / cost implication estimate
- In the current baseline the objective stays at 1.0 after forcing the three on (no change) because acetone was already present.
- In general, enabling the full set {12,18,19} will force reaction 09 and therefore force y[6]=1; if that would change y[6] from 0 to 1 it increases the objective by +1 (discrete jump in this model).

- to make 17, 18, 19 all present now, I can run the model after changing material_cons[19] and enabling y[23] (or y[24]) and report exact variable changes; or
- to allow them but keep acetone off, I can show the specific single break you must keep in reaction 09 (e.g., set y[12]=0 or remove the rm entry for one reactant) and test the outcome.

# Load and solve baseline model, then add new constraint and resolve
from pprint import pprint
import pyomo.environ as pyo

# Helper functions provided by the environment
# - load_model(version, models_dictionary)
# - solve_model(model, version, models_dictionary, tool_context, description)

# 1) Load and solve the baseline model
model_base = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)

desc_base = (
    'Baseline solve: original chem model with no additional what-if constraints. '
    'Purpose: establish feasibility, objective (y[6]), and reference chemical presence for comparison.'
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(model_base, 'chem', models_dictionary, tool_context, desc_base)

# Extract baseline values
base_obj = pyo.value(model_base.obj)
base_y = {int(i): int(pyo.value(model_base.y[i])) for i in model_base.C}

# Function to find reactions that actively produce a given product (all reactants present)
def active_reactions_for_product(model, prod_id:int):
    active = []
    for r in model.R:
        # Collect reactants for this reaction producing prod_id
        reactants = [c for c in model.C if (r, prod_id, c) in model.rm and int(pyo.value(model.rm[r, prod_id, c])) == 1]
        if not reactants:
            continue
        all_present = all(pyo.value(model.y[c]) >= 0.5 for c in reactants)
        if all_present:
            active.append(r)
    return active

base_active_rxns_for_6 = active_reactions_for_product(model_base, 6)

# 2) Load a fresh copy, add the at-most-one constraint, and solve
model_whatif = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)
# Add the new availability cap: y[1] + y[2] + y[3] <= 1
model_whatif.at_most_one_1_2_3 = pyo.Constraint(expr=model_whatif.y[1] + model_whatif.y[2] + model_whatif.y[3] <= 1)

desc_whatif = (
    'What-if analysis: added constraint y[1] + y[2] + y[3] <= 1 to cap availability of chemicals 1,2,3. '
    'Goal: assess feasibility, impact on acetone producibility (y[6]), objective change, reaction pathways, and key presence flips.'
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(model_whatif, 'chem__y_1_2_3_atmost1_set1', models_dictionary, tool_context, desc_whatif)

# Extract post-change values (if solved)
status_whatif = getattr(model_whatif, 'solution_status', None) if hasattr(model_whatif, 'solution_status') else None

whatif_feasible = True
try:
    whatif_obj = pyo.value(model_whatif.obj)
    whatif_y = {int(i): int(pyo.value(model_whatif.y[i])) for i in model_whatif.C}
    whatif_active_rxns_for_6 = active_reactions_for_product(model_whatif, 6)
except Exception as e:
    whatif_feasible = False
    whatif_obj = None
    whatif_y = {}
    whatif_active_rxns_for_6 = []

# Collect reaction identifiers in a uniform printable form
R_list = list(model_base.R)

# Prepare specific reaction status checks for 2,6,8,9
# We will check membership in active reaction list (strings in R may be like '2' or 'r02'; so we map flexibly)

def rxn_status(rxn_id, active_list):
    # Try exact, string, and zero-padded variants
    candidates = [rxn_id, str(rxn_id), f"{int(rxn_id):02d}", f"r{int(rxn_id):02d}", f"R{int(rxn_id):02d}"]
    for cand in candidates:
        if cand in active_list:
            return True, cand
    return False, None

check_rxns = [2,6,8,9]
rxn_check_results_base = {rid: rxn_status(rid, base_active_rxns_for_6) for rid in check_rxns}
rxn_check_results_whatif = {rid: rxn_status(rid, whatif_active_rxns_for_6) for rid in check_rxns}

# Compute flips for key chemicals
key_idxs = [1,2,3,4,5,8,11,12,13,17,18,19,31]
flips = {}
for k in key_idxs:
    before = base_y.get(k, None)
    after = whatif_y.get(k, None) if whatif_feasible else None
    if before is not None and after is not None and before != after:
        flips[k] = (before, after)

# Also compute all flips to mention notable ones
all_flips = {k: (base_y[k], whatif_y[k]) for k in base_y.keys() if k in whatif_y and base_y[k] != whatif_y[k]}

# Build a compact result dictionary to return
result = {
    'baseline_obj': base_obj,
    'whatif_feasible': whatif_feasible,
    'whatif_obj': whatif_obj,
    'y6_baseline': base_y.get(6, None),
    'y6_whatif': whatif_y.get(6, None) if whatif_feasible else None,
    'active_rxns_for_6_baseline': base_active_rxns_for_6,
    'active_rxns_for_6_whatif': whatif_active_rxns_for_6,
    'rxn_check_results_base': rxn_check_results_base,
    'rxn_check_results_whatif': rxn_check_results_whatif,
    'key_flips': flips,
    'all_flips_count': len(all_flips),
    'R_list_sample': R_list[:10],
}
result

import pyomo.environ as pyo

# Load baseline (prefer the latest saved one)
try:
    m_base = load_model('chem_2', models_dictionary)
except Exception:
    m_base = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)

base_obj = pyo.value(m_base.obj)
base_y = {int(i): int(pyo.value(m_base.y[i])) for i in m_base.C}

# Identify which of 1,2,3 are 1 in baseline
base_123 = {i: base_y.get(i, None) for i in [1,2,3]}

# Active reactions for product 6

def active_reactions_for_product(model, prod_id:int):
    active = []
    for r in model.R:
        reactants = [c for c in model.C if (r, prod_id, c) in model.rm and int(pyo.value(model.rm[r, prod_id, c])) == 1]
        if not reactants:
            continue
        if all(pyo.value(model.y[c]) >= 0.5 for c in reactants):
            active.append(r)
    return active

base_active_rxns_for_6 = active_reactions_for_product(m_base, 6)

# Try to load what-if model and check status
m_wi = load_model('chem__y_1_2_3_atmost1_set1', models_dictionary)
status_wi = getattr(m_wi, 'solution_status', None) if hasattr(m_wi, 'solution_status') else None
is_infeasible = (status_wi is not None and 'infeasible' in str(status_wi).lower())

result = {
    'baseline_obj': base_obj,
    'baseline_y6': base_y.get(6),
    'baseline_y_1_2_3': base_123,
    'baseline_active_rxns_for_6': base_active_rxns_for_6,
    'whatif_status': str(status_wi),
    'whatif_infeasible': is_infeasible,
    'baseline_y': base_y,
}

import pyomo.environ as pyo

def get_baseline():
    try:
        m = load_model('chem_2', models_dictionary)
    except Exception:
        m = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)
    return m

m = get_baseline()

base_y = {int(i): int(pyo.value(m.y[i])) for i in m.C}
base_obj = float(pyo.value(m.obj))

# Gather all reactions that can produce product 6 and their reactants
rxn_reactants_for6 = {}
for r in m.R:
    reacts = [int(c) for c in m.C if (r, 6, c) in m.rm and int(pyo.value(m.rm[r,6,c])) == 1]
    if reacts:
        rxn_reactants_for6[str(r)] = reacts

# Identify which of these are active under baseline (all reactants present)
active_r_for6 = []
for r, reacts in rxn_reactants_for6.items():
    if all(base_y[c] == 1 for c in reacts):
        active_r_for6.append(r)

# For each reaction producing 6, count how many of {1,2,3}
counts_123 = {r: sum(1 for c in reacts if c in [1,2,3]) for r, reacts in rxn_reactants_for6.items()}

result = {
    'baseline_obj': base_obj,
    'baseline_y1_y2_y3': {k: base_y.get(k) for k in [1,2,3]},
    'baseline_y6': base_y.get(6),
    'rxns_producing_6': rxn_reactants_for6,
    'active_rxns_for6': active_r_for6,
    'counts_123_in_rxns_for6': counts_123,
}
result

Root cause: The infeasibility is purely due to the conflict between “at most one of {1,2,3}” and the baseline availability constraints that force more than one of these to be 1. So the smallest, practical fixes are about availability, not reactions.

Two minimal paths forward (pick one):

A) Keep the cap (≤ 1), relax availability of two of {1, 2, 3}
- What to change:
  - Make two of these three not “always-available” (i.e., remove them from the fixed-available set so they can be 0). In practice: do not stock two of these simultaneously or treat them as optional resources.
  - Recommended: allow only one of the three to remain fixed-available (or allow all three to be optional), so the sum ≤ 1 can be satisfied.
- Which single one to allow? To retain acetone producibility without relying on the ethyl acetate/sodium ethoxide route (which typically needs more than one of {1,2,3}), prioritize enabling an alternative pathway that does not use these chemicals, for example:
  - Ozonolysis route: If isobutene (y[18]) and ozone (y[19]) are available, acetone can be formed directly via ozonolysis. This route avoids chemicals 1–3 entirely, so you can keep at most one of {1,2,3} without affecting acetone production.
  - Oxidation route: If isopropanol (y[15]) is available along with CrO3 (y[16]) and H2SO4 (y[17]), oxidation to acetone is possible, also avoiding 1–3.
- Expected objective impact:
  - If one of these alternative routes’ reactants are all available, acetone will again be logically forced (y[6] = 1), so the objective would return to 1.0 (same as baseline).
  - If no alternative route has all reactants available, the model (which minimizes y[6]) would prefer y[6] = 0, yielding objective 0.0.

B) Loosen the cap slightly
- If, in your current baseline data, all three of {1,2,3} are forced to 1, then y[1]+y[2]+y[3] ≤ 2 will still be infeasible. The minimum loosening that regains feasibility without changing availabilities would be y[1] + y[2] + y[3] ≤ 3 (i.e., removing the cap for those three). This defeats the purpose of the restriction, so Option A is the meaningful way to keep “≤ 1” and regain feasibility.

Practical parameters you can adjust
- Availability (stocking) status:
  - Chemicals 1–3 (y[1] ethyl acetate, y[2] sodium ethoxide, y[3] ethanol): move two from “mandatory available” to “optional,” so the cap y[1] + y[2] + y[3] ≤ 1 can be satisfied.
  - To enable acetone without using 1–3, ensure availability for one of these alternative pathways:
    - Ozonolysis: y[18] (isobutene) and y[19] (ozone).
    - Oxidation: y[15] (isopropanol), y[16] (CrO3), y[17] (H2SO4).
- Policy choice within the cap:
  - If you must allow exactly one of {1,2,3} to remain available, pick any one; the alternative routes above do not consume 1–3. If you aim to avoid accidental forcing of other products, ethanol (y[3]) as the single allowed might be a practical, benign choice operationally.

Summary
- With y[1] + y[2] + y[3] ≤ 1, the model is infeasible due to fixed availability of more than one among {1,2,3}.
- Baseline objective = 1.0; what-if objective undefined.
- Baseline y[6] = 1; what-if y[6] undefined (no solution).

Short answer
- With the added constraint y[1] + y[2] + y[3] ≤ 1, the model is infeasible — there is no feasible solution. Baseline (no new cap) was feasible with objective = 1.0 (acetone present). The infeasibility comes from a direct conflict between your new cap and existing availability constraints that force more than one of chemicals 1, 2, 3 to be present in the baseline data.

Why it’s infeasible (plain language)
- In the current dataset the model already has more than one of chemicals {1,2,3} fixed as available (y = 1). Forcing the sum ≤ 1 contradicts those fixed availability constraints, so the solver cannot find any assignment that satisfies all constraints. The infeasibility appears before we even check reactions or acetone production.

What this means for acetone
- Because the problem is infeasible there is no valid “after” solution to say whether acetone would be produced. In the baseline acetone is present (y[6] = 1), but under the new cap we cannot compute a solution until the availability conflict is resolved.

Practical ways to restore feasibility (minimal, actionable options)
1) Make two of {1,2,3} optional (recommended)
   - Remove the “forced available” status for two of these chemicals so the model can choose at most one to turn on. Concretely: change the input data so only one (or none) of 1,2,3 is fixed to y=1; let the others be free (0/1).
   - If you do that, the cap y1+y2+y3 ≤ 1 becomes satisfiable and the model will solve.

2) Keep the cap but enable alternative acetone routes (if your goal is to produce acetone without relying on 1–3)
   - Ensure one of the alternative pathways has all its reactants available. Examples:
     - Ozonolysis route: enable isobutene (y[18]) and ozone (y[19]) together (these two plus water produce acetone via reaction 09). If 18 and 19 are present along with water, acetone will be forced (y[6] = 1).
     - Oxidation route: enable isopropanol (y[15]) + CrO3 (y[16]) + H2SO4 (y[17]) (reaction 08 path), which can also produce acetone without using chemicals 1–3.
   - If you enable one of these alternative routes, acetone can still be produced even while selecting at most one of {1,2,3}.

3) Loosen the cap (least desirable if you must limit selection)
   - Change the cap to y1+y2+y3 ≤ 2 or remove it entirely. This restores feasibility without changing availability inputs, but it defeats the “only one allowed” policy.

Expected objective impacts (estimates)
- If you restore feasibility by making two of 1–3 optional but do not enable any alternative acetone route, the solver (which minimizes acetone) will choose y[6] = 0 if possible — objective could drop to 0.0.
- If you restore feasibility and also enable any full acetone-producing route (e.g., 18+19 or 15+16+17), the model will force acetone on again (y[6] = 1) and objective will be 1.0 (same as baseline).
- If you simply relax the availability of two chemicals but keep the rest of baseline unchanged, the model will pick the best single chemical among {1,2,3} consistent with the objective and other constraints.

Concrete next steps I can run for you
- Option A: I can make two of {1,2,3} optional and re-run the model; I will report feasibility, new objective, and whether acetone is produced.
- Option B: I can keep the cap and explicitly enable an alternate acetone route (e.g., set y[18]=1 and y[19]=1) and re-run to show the resulting solution and objective.
- Option C: I can test which single choice among {1,2,3} (keep 1 only, keep 2 only, or keep 3 only) lets you produce acetone under current other availabilities.

# Load, modify, and solve the chem model under a new availability constraint y[3]+y[4]+y[5] <= 1
from pyomo import environ as pyo
from math import isclose

# Helper functions provided
model = load_model('chem', models_dictionary)

# Deactivate conflicting fixed-availability constraints for 3 and 5 to meaningfully apply the new at-most-one rule
if (3,) in model.material_cons:
    model.material_cons[3].deactivate()
if (5,) in model.material_cons:
    model.material_cons[5].deactivate()

# Add the new availability constraint: select at most one of chemicals 3, 4, 5
model.select_at_most_one_3_4_5 = pyo.Constraint(expr = model.y[3] + model.y[4] + model.y[5] <= 1)

# Solve with Gurobi
version_name = 'chem__availability_3_4_5_setLe1'
description = (
    "What-if analysis: imposed a new availability coupling constraint y[3]+y[4]+y[5] <= 1 "
    "and deactivated fixed-availability constraints for y[3] and y[5] to avoid contradiction. "
    "Purpose: test whether acetone (y[6]) can still be produced and identify which reaction pathways remain valid."
)
models_dictionary = solve_model(model, version_name, models_dictionary, tool_context, description)
